The Toeplitz corona theorem for the Euclidean unit ball𝔹n={z∈ℂn:‖z‖<1}\mathbb{B}_{n}=\{z\in\mathbb{C}^{n}:\|z\|<1\}was established in[13]. Givenφ1,…,φd∈H∞​(𝔹n)\varphi_{1},\dotsc,\varphi_{d}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}_{n})andϵ>0\epsilon>0, it
provides necessary and sufficient conditions for the existence of functionsf1,…,fd∈H∞​(𝔹n)f_{1},\dotsc,f_{d}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}_{n})such thatφ1​f1+⋯+φd​fd=1and‖[f1,…,fd]t‖∞,𝔹n≤1ϵ.\varphi_{1}f_{1}+\cdots+\varphi_{d}f_{d}=1\quad\text{and}\quad\|[f_{1},\dotsc,f_{d}]^{t}\|_{\infty,\mathbb{B}_{n}}\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon}.

We now present a different version of the Toeplitz corona theorem for𝔹2\mathbb{B}_{2}which is associated with a vector-valued analog of the classℭ​(𝔹2)\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{B}_{2}). For Hilbert spacesℒ1,ℒ2\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2}, we defineℭ𝔹2​(ℒ1,ℒ2)={g:𝔹2→ℬ​(ℒ1,ℒ2):g​is holomorphic,​‖g​(T1,T2)‖≤1​for all​(T1,T2)∈𝔐𝔹2}.\mathfrak{C}_{\mathbb{B}_{2}}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2})=\{g:\mathbb{B}_{2}\to\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2}):g\ \text{is holomorphic,}\|g(T_{1},T_{2})\|\leq 1\ \text{for all}\ (T_{1},T_{2})\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbb{B}_{2}}\}.

Following the arguments as in(1)⇔(2)(1)\iff(2)of Theorem3.2thatg∈ℭ𝔹2​(ℒ1,ℒ2)g\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathbb{B}_{2}}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2})if and only ifg∘θℍ→𝔹2∈S​Aℍ​(ℒ1,ℒ2)g\circ\theta_{\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{B}_{2}}\in SA_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2}). An application of Theorem2.19gives a Toeplitz corona type theorem for𝔹2\mathbb{B}_{2}as stated below. We leave the proof to the reader.

## 4.Realization, interpolation, extension and Toeplitz corona theorems on𝔼\mathbb{E}

The authors of[39]obtained a realization theorem for functions in the Schur-Agler classS​A​(𝔼)SA(\mathbb{E})for the tetrablock, along with an interpolation theorem on𝔼\mathbb{E}in terms of admissible kernels on𝔼\mathbb{E}, and an extension problem on𝔼\mathbb{E}. Let us recall from Section2that𝔐𝔼\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbb{E}}is the collection of all commuting triples(T2,T3,T4)(T_{2},T_{3},T_{4})such that‖T3‖<1\|T_{3}\|<1and‖Ψ​(α,T2,T3,T4)‖<1\|\Psi(\alpha,T_{2},T_{3},T_{4})\|<1for everyα∈𝔻¯\alpha\in\overline{\mathbb{D}}, whereΨ​(α,z(2),z(3),z(4))=(α​z(4)−z(2))/(α​z(3)−1)\Psi(\alpha,z^{(2)},z^{(3)},z^{(4)})=(\alpha z^{(4)}-z^{(2)})/\penalty 50(\alpha z^{(3)}-1). The Schur-Agler classS​A​(𝔼)SA(\mathbb{E})for𝔼\mathbb{E}is defined asS​A​(𝔼)={f∈Hol​(𝔼):‖f​(T2,T3,T4)‖<1​for all​(T2,T3,T4)∈𝔐𝔼}.SA(\mathbb{E})=\{f\in\text{Hol}(\mathbb{E}):\|f(T_{2},T_{3},T_{4})\|<1\ \text{for all}\ (T_{2},T_{3},T_{4})\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbb{E}}\}.

In this section, we also provide a realization theorem for functions inS​A​(𝔼)SA(\mathbb{E}), which is presented in terms of functions belonging toS​A​(ℍ)SA(\mathbb{H})and admissible kernels onℍ\mathbb{H}. Moreover, we obtain interpolation and extension theorems on𝔼\mathbb{E}as consequences of the corresponding results onℍ\mathbb{H}established in Section2. To begin with, note that(z(1),z(2),z(3))∈𝔼(z^{(1)},z^{(2)},z^{(3)})\in\mathbb{E}if and only if(0,z(1),z(2),z(3))∈ℍ(0,z^{(1)},z^{(2)},z^{(3)})\in\mathbb{H}. Furthermore, ifg∈Hol​(𝔼)g\in\text{Hol}(\mathbb{E}), then the mapg∘θℍ→𝔼g\circ\theta_{\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{E}}is holomorphic onℍ\mathbb{H}, whereθℍ→𝔼:ℍ→𝔼,(z(1),z(2),z(3),z(4))↦(z(2),z(3),z(4)).\theta_{\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{E}}:\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{E},\ (z^{(1)},z^{(2)},z^{(3)},z^{(4)})\mapsto(z^{(2)},z^{(3)},z^{(4)}).

Equipped with this observation, we have the following version of realization theorem on𝔼\mathbb{E}.

## Proof.

The equivalence of(2)(2)with(3)−(5)(3)-(5)follows directly from Theorem2.7. We now prove(1)⟹(2)(1)\implies(2). Letg∈S​A​(𝔼)g\in SA(\mathbb{E})andT¯=(T1,T2,T3,T4)∈𝔐ℍ\underline{T}=(T_{1},T_{2},T_{3},T_{4})\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbb{H}}. Evidently,(0,T2,T3,T4)∈𝔐𝔼(0,T_{2},T_{3},T_{4})\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbb{E}}. Thus,‖g​(T2,T3,T4)‖=‖g∘θℍ→𝔼​(0,T2,T3,T4)‖≤1\|g(T_{2},T_{3},T_{4})\|=\|g\circ\theta_{\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{E}}(0,T_{2},T_{3},T_{4})\|\leq 1and so,g∈S​A​(𝔼)g\in SA(\mathbb{E}).

The following interpolation theorem is obtained as an application of Theorems2.8and4.1.

forzi=(zi(1),zi(2),zi(3))z_{i}=(z_{i}^{(1)},z_{i}^{(2)},z_{i}^{(3)})and1≤i,j≤n1\leq i,j\leq n.

In the case when(1)(1)holds, one may choosef=g∘θℍ→𝔼f=g\circ\theta_{\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{E}}in(2)(2).

## Proof.

The part(1)⟹(2)(1)\implies(2)follows from Theorem4.1by choosingf=g∘θℍ→𝔼f=g\circ\theta_{\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{E}}. The equivalence of(2)(2)with(3)−(4)(3)-(4)follows from Theorem2.8. The authors of[39]established the following extension theorem on𝔼\mathbb{E}. Below, we provide an alternative proof here based on Theorem2.9.

For givenφ1,…,φd∈H∞​(𝔼)\varphi_{1},\dotsc,\varphi_{d}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{E}), the authors of[39]presented necessary and sufficient conditions for the existence off1,…,fd∈H∞​(𝔼)f_{1},\dotsc,f_{d}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{E})such thatφ1​f1+…+φd​fd=1\varphi_{1}f_{1}+\dotsc+\varphi_{d}f_{d}=1withf1,…,fdf_{1},\dotsc,f_{d}subjected to a certain norm bound condition. This is referred to as the Toeplitz corona theorem for𝔼\mathbb{E}. To do so, they introduced the vector-valued Schur-Agler classS​A𝔼​(ℒ1,ℒ2)SA_{\mathbb{E}}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2})that consists of all holomorphic functionsg:𝔼→ℬ​(ℒ1,ℒ2)g:\mathbb{E}\to\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2})such that‖g​(T2,T3,T4)‖≤1\|g(T_{2},T_{3},T_{4})\|\leq 1for every(T2,T3,T4)∈𝔐𝔼(T_{2},T_{3},T_{4})\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbb{E}}. One verifies by arguments similar to those in the proof of(1)⇔(2)(1)\iff(2)of Theorem4.1that a functiong∈S​A𝔼​(ℒ1,ℒ2)g\in SA_{\mathbb{E}}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2})if and only ifg∘θℍ→𝔼∈S​Aℍ​(ℒ1,ℒ2)g\circ\theta_{\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{E}}\in SA_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2}). Hence, Theorem2.19gives the following equivalent formulation of the Toeplitz corona theorem for𝔼\mathbb{E}, where the characterizations are formulated in terms of admissible kernels onℍ\mathbb{H}. The details are left to the reader.

## Theorem 4.5.

Forφ1,…,φd∈H∞​(𝔼)\varphi_{1},\dotsc,\varphi_{d}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{E})andϵ>0\epsilon>0, the following statements are equivalent:
- (1)(1)

there existf1,…,fd∈H∞​(𝔼)f_{1},\dotsc,f_{d}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{E})such that[f1…fd]t∈1ϵ​S​A𝔼​(ℂ,ℂd)\begin{bmatrix}f_{1}&\dotsc&f_{d}\end{bmatrix}^{t}\in\frac{1}{\epsilon}SA_{\mathbb{E}}(\mathbb{C},\mathbb{C}^{d})and∑j=1𝑑​φj​fj=1\overset{d}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\varphi_{j}f_{j}=1;
- (2)(2)

the map(z,w)↦(∑j=1𝑑​φj∘θℍ→𝔼​(z)​φj∘θℍ→𝔼​(w)¯−ϵ2)​k​(z,w)(z,w)\mapsto\left(\overset{d}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\varphi_{j}\circ\theta_{\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{E}}(z)\overline{\varphi_{j}\circ\theta_{\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{E}}(w)}-\epsilon^{2}\right)k(z,w)

onℍ×ℍ\mathbb{H}\times\mathbb{H}is positive semi-definite for everyk∈A​K​(ℍ)k\in AK(\mathbb{H});
- (3)(3)

there existξ∈C​(𝔻¯2)ℍ+,∇∈C​(𝔻¯)ℍ+\xi\in C(\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{2})^{+}_{\mathbb{H}},\nabla\in C(\overline{\mathbb{D}})^{+}_{\mathbb{H}}andδ∈ℂℍ+\delta\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{H}}^{+}such that for allz,w∈ℍz,w\in\mathbb{H},∑j=1𝑑​φj∘θℍ→𝔼​(z)​φj∘θℍ→𝔼​(w)¯−ϵ2\displaystyle\overset{d}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\varphi_{j}\circ\theta_{\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{E}}(z)\overline{\varphi_{j}\circ\theta_{\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{E}}(w)}-\epsilon^{2}=ξ​(z,w)​(1−E​(z)​E​(w)¯)+∇(z,w)​(1−e​(z)​e​(w)¯)+(1−z(3)​w¯(3))​δ​(z,w),\displaystyle=\xi(z,w)(1-E(z)\overline{E(w)})+\nabla(z,w)(1-e(z)\overline{e(w)})+(1-z^{(3)}\overline{w}^{(3)})\delta(z,w),

whereE​(z)E(z)ande​(z)e(z)are as in (2).

Concluding remark.The present work identifies the hexablock as a unifying domain for realization, interpolation, extension and Toeplitz corona theorems. In particular, the Schur-Agler class introduced here not only establishes these results for the hexablock itself but also leads to analogous results for the Euclidean unit ball inℂ2\mathbb{C}^{2}and recovers the corresponding theory for the tetrablock. We expect that this viewpoint will be useful in the investigation of function-theoretic and operator-theoretic questions on other domains associated with structured singular values.
